                anchor [ref=87, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software_release_life_cycle#Beta]
                  text "beta"

                anchor [ref=89, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_Explorer_6]

                  text "15"
                  text "]"

                anchor [ref=91, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_Explorer]
                  text "Internet Explorer"

                anchor [ref=92, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-16]

                  text "16"

              container "DRM content"
                heading
                  text "DRM content"
                anchor [ref=279, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Firefox&action=edit&section=6]
                  text "["
                  text "edit"
                  text "]"
                paragraph
                  text "Firefox has supported playback of video content protected by"
                  anchor [ref=280, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HTML5]
                    text "HTML5"
                  anchor [ref=281, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encrypted_Media_Extensions]
                    text "Encrypted Media Extensions"
                  text "(EME), since version 38"
                  text ". For security and privacy reasons, EME is implemented within a wrapper of open-source code that allows execution of a"
                  anchor [ref=282, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proprietary_software]
                    text "proprietary"
                  anchor [ref=283, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_rights_management]
                    text "DRM"
                  text "module by"
                  anchor [ref=284, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adobe_Systems]
                    text "Adobe Systems"
                  text "—Adobe Primetime Content Decryption Module (CDM). CDM runs within a ""
                  anchor [ref=285, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandbox_(computer_security)]
                    text "sandbox"
                  text "" environment to limit its access to the system and provide it a randomized device ID to prevent services from"
                  anchor [ref=286, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Device_fingerprint]
                    text "uniquely identifying the device"
                  text "for tracking purposes. The DRM module, once it has been downloaded, is enabled and disabled in the same manner as other"
                  anchor [ref=287, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plug-in_(computing)]
                    text "plug-ins"
                  text ". Firefox has supported Google's Widevine CDM on Windows and Mac OS X since version 47."
                  anchor [ref=288, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-64]
                    text "["
                    text "64"
                    text "]"

            container "Security"
              heading
                text "Security"
              anchor [ref=392, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Firefox&action=edit&section=14]
                text "["
                text "edit"
                text "]"
              text "See also:"
              anchor [ref=393, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Browser_security]
                text "Browser security"
              paragraph
                text "From its inception, Firefox was positioned as a security-focused browser. At the time,"
                anchor [ref=394, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_Explorer]
                  text "Internet Explorer"
                text ", the dominant browser, was facing a security crisis. Multiple vulnerabilities had been found, and"
                anchor [ref=395, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malware]
                  text "malware"
                text "like"
                anchor [ref=396, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Download.ject]
                  text "Download.Ject"
                text "could be installed simply by visiting a compromised website. The situation was so severe that the U.S. government reportedly issued warnings about security risks with Internet Explorer."
                anchor [ref=397, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-111]
                  text "["
                  text "111"
                  text "]"
                text "Firefox, being less integrated with the operating system, was considered a safer alternative since it was less likely to have issues that could completely compromise a computer. This led to a signific..."
                anchor [ref=398, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-WSJHowTo2-112]
                  text "["
                  text "112"
                  text "]"
                anchor [ref=399, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-113]
                  text "["
                  text "113"
                  text "]"
                text "Moreover, Firefox was considered to have fewer actively exploitable"
                anchor [ref=400, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_vulnerabilities]
                  text "security vulnerabilities"
                text "compared to its competitors. In 2006,"
                anchor [ref=401, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Washington_Post]
                  text "The Washington Post"
                text "reported that exploit code for known security vulnerabilities in Internet Explorer was available for 284 days compared to only nine days for Firefox before the problem was fixed."
                anchor [ref=402, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-114]
                  text "["
                  text "114"
                  text "]"
                text "A"
                anchor [ref=403, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NortonLifeLock]
                  text "Symantec"
                text "study around the same period showed that even though Firefox had a higher number of vulnerabilities, on average vulnerabilities were fixed faster in Firefox than in other browsers during that period."
                anchor [ref=404, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-115]
                  text "["
                  text "115"
                  text "]"
              paragraph
                text "During this period, Firefox used a"
                anchor [ref=405, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monolithic_system]
                  text "monolithic architecture"
                text ", like most browsers at the time. This meant all browser components ran in a single"
                anchor [ref=406, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Process_(computing)]
                  text "process"
                text "with access to all"
                anchor [ref=407, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/System_resource]
                  text "system resources"
                text ". This setup had multiple security issues. If a web page used too many resources, the entire Firefox process would hang or crash, affecting all tabs. Additionally, any exploit could easily access syst..."
                anchor [ref=408, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-116]
                  text "["
                  text "116"
                  text "]"
                text "However, Firefox was slower to adopt this change. It wasn't until 2015 that Firefox started its Electrolysis (e10s) project to implement sandboxing across multiple components. This rewrite relied on"
                anchor [ref=409, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter-process_communication]
                  text "interprocess communication"
                text "using"
                anchor [ref=410, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chromium_(web_browser)]
                  text "Chromium"
                text "'s interprocess communication library and placed various components, including the rendering component, in its sandbox."
                anchor [ref=411, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-117]
                  text "["
                  text "117"
                  text "]"
                text "Firefox released this rewrite into beta in August 2016, noting a 10–20% increase in memory usage, which was lower than Chrome's at the time."
                anchor [ref=412, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-:0-118]
                  text "["
                  text "118"
                  text "]"
                text "However, the rewrite caused issues with their legacy extension API, which was not designed to work cross-process and required"
                anchor [ref=413, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shim_(computing)]
                  text "shim code"
                text "to function correctly."
                anchor [ref=414, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-:0-118]
                  text "["
                  text "118"
                  text "]"
                text "After over a year in beta, the rewrite was enabled by default for all users of Firefox in November 2017."
                anchor [ref=415, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-119]
                  text "["
                  text "119"
                  text "]"
              paragraph
                text "In 2012, Mozilla launched a new project called"
                anchor [ref=416, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Servo_(software)]
                  text "Servo"
                text "to write a completely new and experimental browser engine utilizing"
                anchor [ref=417, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memory_safety]
                  text "memory-safe"
                text "techniques written in"
                anchor [ref=418, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rust_(programming_language)]
                  text "Rust"

                anchor [ref=422, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-122]
                  text "["
                  text "122"
                  text "]"
                text "Additionally, the older incompatible extension API was removed in favour of a WebExtension API that more closely resembled Google Chrome's extension system. This broke compatibility with older extensi..."
                anchor [ref=423, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-123]

            container "Privacy"
              heading
                text "Privacy"
              anchor [ref=431, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Firefox&action=edit&section=15]
                text "["
                text "edit"
                text "]"
              paragraph
                text "When Firefox was initially released, it used a custom script permission policy where scripts that were signed by the page could gain access to higher privilege actions, such as the ability to set a us..."
                anchor [ref=432, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-origin_policy]
                  text "same-origin policy"
                text "used by most modern browsers. This policy disallows scripts from accessing privileged data, including data from other websites."
                anchor [ref=433, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-128]
                  text "["
                  text "128"
                  text "]"
              paragraph
                text "It uses"
                anchor [ref=434, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_Layer_Security]
                  text "TLS"
                text "to protect communications with web servers using strong"
                anchor [ref=435, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cryptography]
                  text "cryptography"
                text "when using the"
                anchor [ref=436, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HTTPS]
                  text "HTTPS"
                text "protocol."
                anchor [ref=437, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-129]
                  text "["
                  text "129"
                  text "]"
                text "The freely available"
                anchor [ref=438, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HTTPS_Everywhere]
                  text "HTTPS Everywhere"
                text "add-on enforces HTTPS, even if a regular HTTP"
                anchor [ref=439, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/URL]
                  text "URL"
                text "is entered; this feature is now part of the browser."
                anchor [ref=440, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-130]
                  text "["
                  text "130"
                  text "]"
                text "Firefox supports HTTP/2."
                anchor [ref=441, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-131]
                  text "["
                  text "131"
                  text "]"
              paragraph
                text "In February 2013, plans were announced for Firefox 22 to disable"
                anchor [ref=442, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third-party_cookie]
                  text "third-party cookies"
                text "by default. However, the introduction of the feature was then delayed so Mozilla developers could "collect and analyze data on the effect of blocking some third-party cookies." Mozilla also collaborat..."
                anchor [ref=443, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanford_University]
                  text "Stanford University"
                text "'s "Cookie Clearinghouse" project to develop a"
                anchor [ref=444, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blacklist]
                  text "blacklist"
                text "and"
                anchor [ref=445, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitelist]
                  text "whitelist"
                text "of sites that will be used in the filter."
                anchor [ref=446, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-pcmag-tpcookies-132]
                  text "["
                  text "132"
                  text "]"
                anchor [ref=447, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-cw-cookieblocking-133]
                  text "["
                  text "133"
                  text "]"
              paragraph
                text "Version 23, released in August 2013, followed the lead of its competitors by blocking"
                anchor [ref=448, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HTML_element#Frames]
                  text "iframe"
                text ", stylesheet, and script resources served from non-HTTPS servers embedded on HTTPS pages by default. Additionally,"
                anchor [ref=449, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JavaScript]
                  text "JavaScript"
                text "could also no longer be disabled through Firefox's preferences, and JavaScript was automatically re-enabled for users who upgraded to 23 or higher with it disabled. The change was made because JavaScr..."
                anchor [ref=450, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NoScript]
                  text "NoScript"
                text ", which can disable JavaScript in a more controlled fashion, were widely available. The following release added the ability to disable JavaScript through the developer tools for testing purposes."
                anchor [ref=451, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-ff23-relnotes-134]
                  text "["
                  text "134"
                  text "]"
                anchor [ref=452, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-135]
                  text "["
                  text "135"
                  text "]"
                anchor [ref=453, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-136]
                  text "["
                  text "136"
                  text "]"
              paragraph
                text "Beginning with Firefox 48, all extensions must be signed by Mozilla to be used in release and beta versions of Firefox. Firefox 43 blocked unsigned extensions but allowed enforcement of extension sign..."
                anchor [ref=454, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mozilla_Add-ons]
                  text "Mozilla Add-ons"
                text "and be subject to code analysis to be signed, although extensions do not have to be listed on the service to be signed."
                anchor [ref=455, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-137]
                  text "["
                  text "137"
                  text "]"
                anchor [ref=456, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-signing-138]
                  text "["
                  text "138"
                  text "]"
                text "On May 2, 2019, Mozilla announced that it would be strengthening the signature enforcement with methods that included the retroactive disabling of old extensions now deemed to be insecure."
                anchor [ref=457, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-139]
                  text "["
                  text "139"
                  text "]"
              paragraph
                text "Since version 60, Firefox includes the option to use"
                anchor [ref=458, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DNS_over_HTTPS]
                  text "DNS over HTTPS"
                text "(DoH), which causes"
                anchor [ref=459, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domain_Name_System]
                  text "DNS lookup"
                text "requests to be sent encrypted over the HTTPS protocol."
                anchor [ref=460, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-140]
                  text "["
                  text "140"
                  text "]"
                anchor [ref=461, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-141]
                  text "["
                  text "141"
                  text "]"
                text "To use this feature the user must set certain preferences beginning with "network.trr" (Trusted Recursive Resolver) in"
                anchor [ref=462, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/About:config]
                  text "about:config"
                text ": if network.trr.mode is 0, DoH is disabled; 1 activates DoH in addition to unencrypted DNS; 2 causes DoH to be used before unencrypted DNS; to use only DoH, the value must be 3. By setting network.tr..."
                anchor [ref=463, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cloudflare]
                  text "Cloudflare"
                text "servers will be activated. Mozilla has a privacy agreement with this server host that restricts its collection of information about incoming DNS requests."
                anchor [ref=464, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-142]
                  text "["
                  text "142"
                  text "]"
              paragraph
                text "On May 21, 2019, Firefox was updated to include the ability to block scripts that used a computer's"
                anchor [ref=465, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CPU]
                  text "CPU"
                text "to mine"
                anchor [ref=466, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cryptocurrency]
                  text "cryptocurrency"
                text "without a user's permission, in Firefox version 67.0. The update also allowed users to block known"
                anchor [ref=467, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Device_fingerprint]
                  text "fingerprinting"
                text "scripts that track their activity across the web, however it does not resist fingerprinting on its own."
                anchor [ref=468, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-143]
                  text "["
                  text "143"
                  text "]"
                text "In February 2021, Firefox launched Total Cookie Protection in version 86 to offer protection against"
                anchor [ref=469, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cross-site_tracking]
                  text "cross-site tracking"
                text ", without breaking the websites users visit."
                anchor [ref=470, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-144]
                  text "["
                  text "144"
                  text "]"
                text "Also known as state partitioning, Total Cookie Protection works by creating a separate "cookie jar" for each website, thereby preventing data from being shared between websites. The feature, which was..."
                anchor [ref=471, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_Science]
                  text "Popular Science"
                text ","
                anchor [ref=472, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-145]
                  text "["
                  text "145"
                  text "]"
                text "also isolates local storage, service workers, and other common ways for sites to store data."
                anchor [ref=473, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-146]
                  text "["
                  text "146"
                  text "]"
                text "Total Cookie Protection was enabled by default in 2022."
                anchor [ref=474, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-147]
                  text "["
                  text "147"
                  text "]"

                  text "["
                  text "149"
                  text "]"

                  text "["

                  text "["

                text "2026"

                text "the supported versions of Firefox are available in 97 locales (88 languages)."

                paragraph
                  text "Firefox 1.0 was released for"
                  anchor [ref=681, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windows_95]
                    text "Windows 95"
                  text ", as well as"
                  anchor [ref=682, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windows_NT_4.0]
                    text "Windows NT 4.0"
                  text "or later. Some users reported the 1.x builds were operable (but not installable) on"
                  anchor [ref=683, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windows_NT_3.51]
                    text "Windows NT 3.51"
                  text "."
                  anchor [ref=684, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-184]
                    text "["
                    text "184"
                    text "]"
                paragraph
                  text "The version 42.0 release includes the first"
                  anchor [ref=685, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X64]
                    text "x64"
                  text "build. It required"
                  anchor [ref=686, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windows_7]
                    text "Windows 7"
                  text "or"
                  anchor [ref=687, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Server_2008_R2]
                    text "Server 2008 R2"
                  text "."
                  anchor [ref=688, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-185]
                    text "["
                    text "185"
                    text "]"
                  text "Starting from version 49.0, Firefox for Windows requires and uses the"
                  anchor [ref=689, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SSE2]
                    text "SSE2"
                  text "instruction set."
                paragraph
                  text "In September 2013, Mozilla released a"
                  anchor [ref=690, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_Windows_Platform_apps]
                    text "Metro-style version"
                  text "of Firefox, optimized for"
                  anchor [ref=691, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Touchscreen]
                    text "touchscreen"
                  text "use, on the "Aurora" release channel. However, on March 14, 2014, Mozilla cancelled the project because of a lack of user adoption."
                  anchor [ref=692, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-fx-metrocancelled-186]
                    text "["
                    text "186"
                    text "]"
                  anchor [ref=693, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-mozandroid-devices-187]
                    text "["
                    text "187"
                    text "]"
                  anchor [ref=694, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-engadget-firefoxmetro-188]
                    text "["
                    text "188"
                    text "]"
                paragraph
                  text "In March 2017, Firefox 52 ESR, the last version of the browser for"

                  anchor [ref=717, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_binary]

                  text "Starting with version 4.0, Firefox was released for the x64 architecture, to which macOS had migrated."

                heading

                paragraph
                  text "Firefox for mobile, code-named "Fennec", was first released for"
                  anchor [ref=752, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maemo]
                    text "Maemo"
                  text "in January 2010 with version 1.0"
                  anchor [ref=753, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-215]
                    text "["
                    text "215"
                    text "]"
                  text "and for"
                  anchor [ref=754, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Android_(operating_system)]
                    text "Android"

                  anchor [ref=755, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-ff43-216]
                    text "["
                    text "216"
                    text "]"
                  text "Support for Maemo was discontinued after version 7, released in September 2011."
                  anchor [ref=756, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-217]
                    text "["
                    text "217"
                    text "]"
                  text "Fennec had a user interface optimized for phones and tablets. It included the Awesome Bar, tabbed browsing, add-on support, a"
                  anchor [ref=757, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Password_manager]
                    text "password manager"
                  text ", location-aware browsing, and the ability to synchronize with the user's other devices with Mozilla Firefox using Firefox Sync (formerly Weave)."
                  anchor [ref=758, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-218]
                    text "["
                    text "218"
                    text "]"
                  anchor [ref=759, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-219]
                    text "["
                    text "219"
                    text "]"
                  text "At the end of its existence, it had a market share of 0.5% on Android."
                  anchor [ref=760, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-220]
                    text "["
                    text "220"
                    text "]"
                paragraph
                  text "In August 2020, Mozilla launched a new version of its Firefox for Android app, named Firefox Daylight to the public"
                  anchor [ref=761, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-Zare-221]
                    text "["
                    text "221"
                    text "]"
                  text "and codenamed"
                  anchor [ref=762, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox_for_Android]
                    text "Fenix"
                  text ","
                  anchor [ref=763, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-222]
                    text "["
                    text "222"
                    text "]"
                  text "after a little over a year of testing."
                  anchor [ref=764, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-auto-223]
                    text "["
                    text "223"
                    text "]"
                  text "It boasted higher speeds with its new"
                  anchor [ref=765, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GeckoView]
                    text "GeckoView"
                  text "engine, which is described as being "the only independent"
                  anchor [ref=766, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Browser_engine]
                    text "web engine browser"
                  text "available on"
                  anchor [ref=767, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Android_(operating_system)]
                    text "Android"
                  text "". It also added Enhanced Tracking Protection 2.0, a feature that blocks many known"
                  anchor [ref=768, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_tracking]
                    text "trackers"
                  text "on the Internet."
                  anchor [ref=769, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-224]
                    text "["
                    text "224"
                    text "]"
                  text "It also added the ability to place the"
                  anchor [ref=770, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Address_bar]
                    text "address bar"
                  text "on the bottom, and a new Collections feature."
                  anchor [ref=771, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-Zare-221]
                    text "["
                    text "221"
                    text "]"
                  text "However, it was criticized for only having nine"
                  anchor [ref=772, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Add-ons_for_Firefox]

                      text "237"

                    text "rendering engine. The"
                    anchor [ref=858, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IOS]

                    text "app uses the operating system's rendering engine ("
                    anchor [ref=859, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WebKit]

              container "Firefox for iOS"
                heading
                  text "Firefox for iOS"

                        image
                  text "Firefox 144 on"
                  anchor [ref=862, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IOS]
                    text "iOS"
                paragraph
                  text "Mozilla initially refused to port Firefox to iOS due to the restrictions Apple imposed on third-party iOS browsers. Instead of releasing a full version of the Firefox browser, Mozilla released Firefox..."
                  anchor [ref=863, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-238]
                    text "["
                    text "238"
                    text "]"
                  anchor [ref=864, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-239]
                    text "["
                    text "239"
                    text "]"
                  text "Mozilla pulled Firefox Home from the"
                  anchor [ref=865, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/App_Store_(Apple)]
                    text "App Store"
                  text "in September 2012, stating it would focus its resources on other projects. The company subsequently released the"
                  anchor [ref=866, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Source_code]
                    text "source code"
                  text "of Firefox Home's underlying synchronization software."
                  anchor [ref=867, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-CW-240]
                    text "["
                    text "240"
                    text "]"
                paragraph
                  text "In April 2013, then-Mozilla CEO"
                  anchor [ref=868, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary_Kovacs]
                    text "Gary Kovacs"
                  text "said that Firefox would not come to iOS if Apple required the use of the"
                  anchor [ref=869, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WebKit]
                    text "WebKit"
                  text "layout engine to do so. One reason given by Mozilla was that before"
                  anchor [ref=870, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IOS_8]
                    text "iOS 8"
                  text ", Apple had supplied third-party browsers with an inferior version of its JavaScript engine, which hobbled their performance, making it impossible to match Safari's JavaScript performance on the iOS p..."
                  anchor [ref=871, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-241]
                    text "["
                    text "241"
                    text "]"
                  text "Apple later opened their "Nitro" JavaScript engine to third-party browsers."
                  anchor [ref=872, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-242]
                    text "["
                    text "242"
                    text "]"
                  text "In 2015, Mozilla announced it was moving forward with Firefox for iOS, with a preview release made available in New Zealand in September of that year."
                  anchor [ref=873, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-243]
                    text "["
                    text "243"
                    text "]"
                  anchor [ref=874, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-244]
                    text "["
                    text "244"
                    text "]"
                  anchor [ref=875, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-245]
                    text "["
                    text "245"
                    text "]"
                  text "It was fully released in November later that year."
                  anchor [ref=876, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-246]
                    text "["
                    text "246"
                    text "]"
                  text "It is the first Firefox-branded browser not to use the"
                  anchor [ref=877, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gecko_(layout_engine)]
                    text "Gecko"
                  anchor [ref=878, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Browser_engine]
                    text "layout engine"
                  text "as is used in Firefox for"
                  anchor [ref=879, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mozilla_Firefox]
                    text "desktop"
                  text "and"
                  anchor [ref=880, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox_for_mobile]
                    text "mobile"
                  text ". Apple's policies require all iOS apps that browse the web to use the built-in WebKit rendering framework and WebKit JavaScript, so using Gecko is not possible."
                  anchor [ref=881, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-247]
                    text "["
                    text "247"
                    text "]"
                  anchor [ref=882, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-248]
                    text "["
                    text "248"
                    text "]"
                  text "Unlike"
                  anchor [ref=883, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox_for_Android]
                    text "Firefox on Android"
                  text ", Firefox for iOS does not support browser add-ons."
                paragraph
                  text "In November 2016, Firefox released a new iOS app titled"
                  anchor [ref=884, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox_Focus]

                anchor [ref=972, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Add-on_(Mozilla)]
                  text "add-on"
                text "compatibility), but that it would help accelerate Firefox's adoption of new web standards, features, and performance improvements."

                  text "["
                  text "272"
                  text "]"
                text "The release process was split into four "channels", with major releases trickling down to the next channel every six to eight weeks. For example, the Nightly channel would feature a preliminary unstab..."
                anchor [ref=976, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-auto1-273]
                  text "["
                  text "273"

                  text "270"
                  text "]"
                anchor [ref=978, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-274]
                  text "["
                  text "274"
                  text "]"
                text "For corporations, Mozilla introduced an Extended Support Release (ESR) channel, with new versions released every 30 weeks (and supported for 12 more weeks after a new ESR version is released), though ..."
                anchor [ref=979, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Backporting]
                  text "backported"
                text "for high-impact vulnerabilities."
                anchor [ref=980, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-275]

            container "Licensing"
              heading
                text "Licensing"
              anchor [ref=991, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Firefox&action=edit&section=26]
                text "["
                text "edit"
                text "]"
              paragraph
                text "Firefox"
                anchor [ref=992, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Source_code]
                  text "source code"
                text "is"
                anchor [ref=993, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_software]
                  text "free software"
                text ", with most of it being released under the"
                anchor [ref=994, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mozilla_Public_License]
                  text "Mozilla Public License"
                text "(MPL) version 2.0."
                anchor [ref=995, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-Licensing-Policies-11]
                  text "["
                  text "11"
                  text "]"
                text "This license permits anyone to view, modify, or redistribute the source code. As a result, several publicly released applications have been built from it, including Firefox's predecessor"
                anchor [ref=996, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netscape_(web_browser)]
                  text "Netscape"
                text ","
                anchor [ref=997, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-283]
                  text "["
                  text "283"
                  text "]"
                text "the customizable"
                anchor [ref=998, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pale_Moon]
                  text "Pale Moon"
                text ", and the privacy focused"
                anchor [ref=999, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tor_Browser]
                  text "Tor Browser"
                text "."
                anchor [ref=1000, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-284]
                  text "["
                  text "284"
                  text "]"
              paragraph
                text "In the past, Firefox was licensed solely under the MPL, then version 1.1,"
                anchor [ref=1001, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-mozrelicensing-285]
                  text "["
                  text "285"
                  text "]"
                text "which the"
                anchor [ref=1002, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Software_Foundation]
                  text "Free Software Foundation"
                text "criticized for being"
                anchor [ref=1003, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weak_copyleft]
                  text "weak copyleft"
                text ", as the license permitted, in limited ways, proprietary"
                anchor [ref=1004, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derivative_work]
                  text "derivative works"
                text ". Additionally, code only licensed under MPL 1.1 could not legally be linked with code under the"
                anchor [ref=1005, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GPL]
                  text "GPL"
                text "."
                anchor [ref=1006, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-286]
                  text "["
                  text "286"
                  text "]"
                anchor [ref=1007, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-287]
                  text "["
                  text "287"
                  text "]"
                text "To address these concerns, Mozilla re-licensed most of Firefox under the"
                anchor [ref=1008, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tri-license]
                  text "tri-license"
                text "scheme of MPL 1.1, GPL 2.0, or"
                anchor [ref=1009, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGPL]
                  text "LGPL"
                text "2.1. Since the re-licensing, developers were free to choose the license under which they received most of the code, to suit their intended use: GPL or LGPL linking and derivative works when one of tho..."
                anchor [ref=1010, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-mozrelicensing-285]
                  text "["
                  text "285"
                  text "]"
                text "However, Mozilla released the GPL-compatible MPL 2.0"
                anchor [ref=1011, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-288]
                  text "["
                  text "288"
                  text "]"
                text "on January 3, 2012,"
                anchor [ref=1012, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-289]
                  text "["
                  text "289"
                  text "]"
                text "and with the release of Firefox 13 on June 5, 2012, Mozilla used it to replace the tri-licensing scheme."
                anchor [ref=1013, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-290]
                  text "["
                  text "290"
                  text "]"

                  text "registered trademark"
                text "of Mozilla; along with the official Firefox logo, it may only be used under certain terms and conditions. Anyone may redistribute the official binaries in unmodified form and use the Firefox name and ..."

                table_row
                  table_cell
                    anchor [ref=1071, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Iceweasel_icon.svg]
                      image
                text "Logo used for"
                anchor [ref=1072, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iceweasel]
                  text "Iceweasel"
              paragraph
                text "There has been some controversy over the Mozilla Foundation's intentions in stopping certain open-source distributions from using the "Firefox" trademark."
                anchor [ref=1073, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-LWNtrademark-12]
                  text "["
                  text "12"
                  text "]"
                text "Open-source browsers "enable greater choice and innovation in the market rather than aiming for mass-market domination.""
                anchor [ref=1074, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-Krishnamurthy-2009-310]
                  text "["
                  text "310"
                  text "]"
                text "Mozilla Foundation Chairperson"
                anchor [ref=1075, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitchell_Baker]
                  text "Mitchell Baker"
                text "explained in an interview in 2007 that distributions could freely use the Firefox trademark if they did not modify source code, and that the Mozilla Foundation's only concern was with users getting a ..."
                anchor [ref=1076, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-311]
                  text "["
                  text "311"
                  text "]"
              paragraph
                text "To allow distributions of the code"
                text "without"
                text "using the official branding, the Firefox"
                anchor [ref=1077, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Build_system]
                  text "build system"
                text "contains a "branding switch". This switch, often used for alphas ("Auroras") of future Firefox versions, allows the code to be compiled without the official logo and name and can allow a derivative wo..."
                text "["
                anchor [ref=1078, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed]
                  text "citation needed"
                text "]"
              paragraph
                text "Distributing modified versions of Firefox under the "Firefox" name required explicit approval from Mozilla for the changes made to the underlying code, and required the use of"
                text "all"
                text "of the official branding. For example, it was not permissible to use the name "Firefox" without also using the official logo. When the"
                anchor [ref=1079, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Debian]
                  text "Debian"
                text "project decided to stop using the official Firefox logo in 2006 (because Mozilla's copyright restrictions at the time were incompatible with"
                anchor [ref=1080, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Debian_Free_Software_Guidelines]
                  text "Debian's guidelines"
                text "), they were told by a representative of the Mozilla Foundation that this was not acceptable and was asked either to comply with the published trademark guidelines or cease using the "Firefox" name in..."
                anchor [ref=1081, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefox#cite_note-312]
                  text "["
                  text "312"
                  text "]"
                text "Debian switched to branding their modified version of Firefox ""
                anchor [ref=1082, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iceweasel]
                  text "Iceweasel"
                text "" (but in 2016 switched back to Firefox), along with other Mozilla software."
                anchor [ref=1083, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GNU_IceCat]
                  text "GNU IceCat"
                text "is another derived version of Firefox distributed by the"
                anchor [ref=1084, url=https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GNU_Project]
                  text "GNU Project"
